OVERVIEW
ureau of Indian Standards (BIS), the National Standards Body of India was 
established under the BIS Act 1986, assuming the functions of the erstwhile 
Indian Standards Institution (ISI). BIS Act 2016 came into force on 12 October 2017 
superseding BIS Act 1986. BIS Act 2016 provides for the establishment of a national 
standards body for the harmonious development of the activities of standardization, 
conformity assessment and quality assurance of goods, articles, processes, systems 
and services and for matters connected therewith or incidental thereto. 
With the integration of global economies, countries around the world are strategically 
positioning themselves at international level to gain competitive edge and achieve 
economic benefits. As standards and conformity assessment are significantly influencing 
global trade, countries are therefore adopting strategies to seek positions in international 
standardization fora to influence international standards in their favour. India in tandem 
with National standardization strategies is interacting with various industry associations 
to harness Indian skills and strength and review the existing standards and make forays 
into new avenues relevant from the view point of standardization.
Standardization supports economic growth, enhances competitiveness and fosters 
technological developments. It is also a tool to influence public policies such as health 
and safety, protection of environment and consumer protection. By ensuring a level 
playing field and defining common criteria of acceptance of goods, standards are 
increasingly being used as tools to support regulatory framework and to facilitate trade. 
In this regard, BIS is fruitfully interacting with various stakeholders to bring a number 
of products under mandatory certification thereby ensuring protection of consumers’ 
interest.
BIS through its core activities of standardization and conformity assessment, has been 
benefiting the national economy by providing safe, reliable and quality goods; minimizing 
health hazards to consumers; protecting the environment, promoting exports and imports 
substitute; controlling over proliferation of varieties etc. The standards and certification 
scheme of BIS apart from benefitting the consumers and industry also support various 
public policies especially in areas of product safety, consumer protection, food safety, 
environment protection, building and construction, etc.
During the year, BIS worked towards specifically addressing various national priorities 
and other Govt. initiatives in the areas of formulation of standards in twelve champion 
service sectors, medical textiles, testing of quality of piped drinking water, mandatory 
hallmarking of gold jewellery to name a few. In standards development, BIS continued to 
address issues of technology changes and advancements, climate change, environment 
and energy conservation, conditions of health and safety and facilitation of trade. In the 
area of conformity assessment BIS worked towards making the processes simpler and 
faster. BIS maintained a satisfactory progress in all its activities during the year.

STATUTORY FRAMEWORK
BIS operates under the framework of the BIS Act of 2016 
and the Rules and Regulations framed there under. The 
Bureau of Indian Standards Act, 2016, was published for 
general information in official gazette on 22 March 2016 
and came into force with effect from 12 October 2017. 
GOVERNANCE 
The Governing 
Council and the 
Executive Committee 
provides the policy 
directives and strategic 
directions and oversees 
the development and 
implementation of 
the organization’s 
objectives and 
goals.
GOVERNING COUNCIL
As per the provisions of the BIS Act, 
2016, the Bureau shall be a body 
corporate. The members of the Governing 
Council 
constitute 
the 
Bureau. 
The 
Governing Council consists of 27 members 
representing the Members of Parliament, 
the Central and the State Governments, 
Industry, Scientific and Research Institutions, 
Consumer Organizations, Professional Bodies, 
Regulatory Bodies and National Accreditation 
Bodies, with the Union Minister of Consumer 
Affairs, Food and Public Distribution as 
its President and the Minister of State 
for Consumer Affairs, Food and Public 
Distribution as its Vice-President.
EXECUTIVE COMMITTEE 
The Executive Committee consists 
of the Director General as ex-
officio Chairman and thirteen other 
members appointed with the prior 
approval of the Central Government. 
The Executive Committee performs, 
exercises 
and 
discharges 
such 
functions, powers and duties of the 
Bureau as delegated by the Governing 
Council. Four meetings of the 
Executive Committee were held 
during the year on 25 June 2019, 30 
October 2019, 05 December 2019 
and 20 February 2020.

ORGANIZATIONAL 
NETWORK
BIS has its Headquarters at New Delhi. It has 5 Regional Offices (ROs) located at Kolkata (Eastern), 
Mumbai (Western), Chandigarh (Northern), Chennai (Southern), and Delhi (Central). Under the 
Regional Offices are the Branch Offices (BOs). There are 32 BOs located at 28 different locations 
namely Ahmedabad, Bengaluru, Bhubaneswar, Bhopal, Chandigarh, Chennai, Coimbatore, 
Dehradun, Delhi, Faridabad, Ghaziabad, Guwahati, Hubli, Hyderabad, Jaipur, Jammu, 
Jamshedpur, Kochi, Kolkata, Lucknow, Mumbai, Nagpur, Parwanoo, Patna, Pune, Raipur, Rajkot 
and Vishakhapatnam. The BOs serve as an effective link between State Governments, industries, 
technical institutions, consumer organizations, etc., of the region. 
To cater to the needs of testing of samples, BIS has eight laboratories in the country namely 
Central Laboratory (CL), Sahibabad; Eastern Regional Office Laboratory (EROL), Kolkata; Western 
Regional Office Laboratory (WROL), Mumbai; Northern Regional Office Laboratory (NROL), 
Mohali; Southern Regional Office Laboratory (SROL), Chennai; Bangalore Branch Office Laboratory 
(BNBOL), Bengaluru; Patna Branch Office Laboratory (PBOL), 
Patna; Guwahati Branch Office Laboratory (GBOL), 
Guwahati.

STANDARDIZATION
STANDARDS FORMULATION
As the National Standards Body, BIS develops Indian Standards through a consultative mechanism in 
technical committees comprising of various stakeholders that have interest in the relevant subject so 
that views of all are given due consideration and a consensus is evolved while formulating a standard. The 
stakeholders can broadly be categorized as industry, consumers/users, technologists (R&D and scientific 
institutions, academia, etc) and government departments/regulators. The process of standard development 
follows accepted international best practices which are based on principles of openness, transparency, 
impartiality and consensus. Apart from consultation within the technical committees, draft standards are 
also open for public views/comments. A standard by definition is a voluntary document and therefore its 
implementation is voluntary unless its compliance is mandated through a regulation or when it forms a part 
of a contract between two parties.
BIS also represents India in international standards bodies like International Organization for Standardization 
(ISO) and International Electrotechnical Commission (IEC) and participates actively in the international 
standardization work undertaken in these bodies. BIS presents the national viewpoints on various draft 
international standards during the process of development of these standards so that the country’s interest 
is protected and reflected in these standards. This also enables the BIS technical committees to consider 
adoption of the International standards as Indian Standards with or without modifications, in order to enable 
our products and services to integrate with global trade and commerce.
There are around 1000 technical committees in BIS carrying out standardization work in 15 broad 
technology areas/sectors. 
REVIEW OF STANDARDS
Indian Standards are reviewed as and when considered necessary, but at least once in five years to 
establish whether these standards are still relevant and up to date with state of the art and to take 
appropriate action for their reaffirmation, revision, issuing amendments, declare obsolescence or 
withdrawal.
HARMONIZATION
In the present market scenario, India is facing the challenge of global competition. To sustain in the global 
markets it is important to harmonize Indian Standards, as far as possible, with International Standards 
formulated by International Organization for Standardization (ISO) and the International Electro-technical 
Commission (IEC). Further, India is a signatory to the WTO Agreement on Technical Barriers to Trade 
(TBT). As per the agreement, member countries of WTO are required to align their National Standards with 
International Standards. However, country specific concerns on health, safety, environment, national 
security and prevention of deceptive practices can be considered/ incorporated while formulating National 
Standards. BIS uses International Standards, wherever they exist as a basis for standards development. 
PUBLICATION OF STANDARDS
BIS, through its publications department, handles the electronic publishing of Indian Standards and their 
amendments and the notification of published standards and amendments in the official Gazette of Govt. of 
India.  During 2019-20, BIS published 1091 (647 new & 444 revised) standards and 145 amendments.
During April 1, 2019 – March 31, 2020, 978 
standards were formulated. The total number of 
Indian Standards in force, as on 31 March 2020 
was 20845. A total of 6169 Indian Standards 
have so far been harmonized with International 
Standards of which 305 Indian Standards were 
harmonized during 2019-20.

on Pashmina is to preserve the culture and heritage 
of Pashmina produced in Leh and Ladakh area and 
also to curb the practice of adulteration of Pashmina 
products available in the market. Certification & 
Labelling of Pashmina products against the Indian 
Standards will ensure availability of genuine 
Pashmina products.

Services sector is a key driver in the economic 
growth of both the developed and developing 
countries with significant contribution in Trade, 
GDP, Employment and Foreign Investments. India 
is a growing services powerhouse of the world as 
the world’s largest outsourcing destination for IT & 
IT enabled services, a popular medical tourism spot, 
growing infrastructure and construction services 
market and many more. 
In parallel with such growth, the services sector is 
in vital need of standards to establish good practice, 
encourage consistently high service quality and build 
consumer confidence. To give due importance to the 
service sector, BIS has established a new Division 
Council in the field of Services sector (Services Sector 
Division Council, SSDC) to exclusively deal with 
Standardization in the Services Sector, especially, 
the 12 Champion Services Sector identified in the 
Indian National Strategy for Standardization (INSS) 
brought out by the Department of Commerce, 
Ministry of Commerce and Industry.
First meeting of Service Sector Division Council 
(SSDC) was held on 25 February 2020 and following 
14 Sectional Committees were constituted, namely


& 
Logistics

Sectional 
Committee, SSD 01


Committee, SSD 02

&

Service Standardization process, letters were sent 
to all stakeholders (Ministries/ State Governments/ 
Industries/ National Institutes) emphasizing the 
importance of services standards, creation of 
Standardization cells and partnership with the 
academic/ research institutions of repute.

Important Standards Revised during 2019-2020
During April 1, 2019 – March 31, 2020, 4159 new 
licences were granted, which include 40 products 
covered for the first time under the scheme. 
The total number of Indian Standards which 
have been covered under BIS Certification Marks 
Scheme is 976. The total number of operative 
licences held by domestic manufacturers as on 
31 March, 2020 was 35623 and 1089 licences 
were held by Foreign Manufacturers
CERTIFICATION 
PRODUCT CERTIFICATION
BIS operates a Product Certification Scheme 
under the Bureau of Indian Standards Act, 
2016 and the Rules there under and BIS 
(Conformity Assessment) Regulations, 2018. 
The presence of Standard Mark (popularly 
known as ISI mark) on a product indicates 
its conformity to the relevant Indian 
Standard. Before granting a licence to any 
manufacturer, BIS ascertains the availability 
of required infrastructure and capability of 
the manufacturer to produce and test the 
product conforming to the relevant Indian 
Standard. Samples drawn from the production 
line as well as from the market are tested in 
BIS laboratories/ recognized independent 
laboratories to ensure conformance of the 
product to the relevant Indian Standard. BIS 
also conducts surveillance visits to ensure 
conformance of licensees’ products to the 
relevant Indian Standard. The certification 
scheme is voluntary in nature, except for 
211 product standards that have been made 
mandatory by the Central Government on 
considerations of health and safety of the 
consumer.

Product Certification Scheme

FOREIGN MANUFACTURES 
CERTIFICATION SCHEME (FMCS)
BIS has been operating separate Scheme 
for foreign manufacturers since year 2000. 
Under this Scheme, foreign manufacturers 
can seek certification from BIS for use of 
BIS Standard Mark on their product(s). 
During 2019-20, 231 licences have been 
issued under FMCS, taking the total 
number of operative licences to 1089 for 
117 Indian Standards.  The licences granted 
cover various products such as steel & 
steel products, cement, tyres & tubes for 
automobile vehicles, plastic/glass feeding 
bottles, electric cables, switchgear products, 
plugs & socket outlets and switches, electrical 
energy meters, electrical appliances, motors, 
capacitors, HDPE & UPVC pipes, infant 
formula, caustic soda, compressors, float 
glass, mineral water etc.,  in around 51 
countries across the world.
During the year FMCS has granted first time 
licences for “Tool and Die Steel” for which 
there is no licence in India as on date. This 
product is used for cold work and hot working 
application capable of being hardened and 
tempered.

COMPULSORY REGISTRATION SCHEME 
(CRS) 
The introduction of CRS is an alternative 
mechanism to the Compulsory Certification 
to facilitate growth of fast growing sectors 
like IT and protect consumers from spurious 
and sub-standard products manufactured in 
India or abroad. The Scheme envisages that 
no person shall manufacture or import or sell 
or distribute goods which do not conform to 
the Specified Standards and do not bear the 
Standard Mark along with the Registration 
number. 
The process of Registration has been re-
engineered and revamped to make it SMART.
SMART Registration is aimed to make the 
process of Registration fast, transparent and 
completely paperless with a click of button. 
SMART registration will reduce the efforts 
and stationery cost involved in Registration 
process both at BIS as well as applicants' end. 
Further going paperless will have a positive 
impact on environment.  In addition to it, the 
menace of maintaining hard copies will be 
avoided and thus, BIS is becoming a part of 
green IT revolution.
The major products under the Scheme are:
	
LED fixtures, lamps, drivers, Flood 
Lights, Hand lamps, and Lighting Chains
	
Recessed 
LED 
Luminaires, 
LED 
Luminaires for Road and Street lighting, 
Luminaires for Emergency Lighting 
	
Mobile 
phones, 
power 
banks 
for 
portable applications, smart watches
	
Rechargeable cells/batteries
	
UPS and invertors of capacity 10 kVA 
and below
	
Microwave ovens
	
Plasma/LCD/LED TVs, Visual display 
units/monitors


products, and for household & similar 
electrical appliances
	
Point-of-sale terminals, ADP machine






The first Registration was granted by BIS 
on 12 June, 2013. As on 31 March 2020, 
BIS has granted 22260 registrations to 
manufacturers located in various countries 
and had approximately 15500 operative 
licences located in various countries including 
India. 
'Orientation Programme for the Authorized Indian Representatives'
The registration work in BIS has become fully 
online. The smart registration procedure was 
inaugurated by Shri A.P Sawhney, Secretary 
MeitY on 03 April 2019
BIS has established eight laboratories in the country 
to cater to the testing needs of samples generated 
from various conformity assessment schemes of 
BIS, beginning with the establishment of the Central 
Laboratory at Sahibabad in 1962. Subsequently, four 
regional laboratories at Mohali, Kolkata, Mumbai 
and Chennai and three branch office laboratories at 
Patna, Bengaluru, and Guwahati were established. 
BIS laboratories have facilities for testing of products 
in the fields of chemical, microbiological, electrical 
and mechanical disciplines. Laboratories at Mumbai, 
Kolkata, Chennai, Mohali, and Sahibabad have been 
accredited by the National Accreditation Board for 
Testing and Calibration Laboratories (NABL).
BIS labs have complete test facilities for testing as 
per 398 Indian Standards and, in addition, partial test 
facilities are also available for 320 Indian Standards. 
During the year, the BIS laboratories have tested 
18853 samples of various products covered under 
Certification. In addition, the Gold Referral Assaying 
Laboratories at Chennai, Sahibabad and Kolkata have 
tested 5506 hallmarking samples during the year. 
Creation/ Upgradation of Testing Facilities
BIS Laboratories are making continuous efforts to 
upgrade the testing facilities available for products 
covered under the conformity assessment schemes 
of BIS with a special emphasis on the products 
covered under mandatory certification. During 
2019-20 under the modernization project, NROL 
(Northern Regional Office Laboratory, Mohali) has 
created complete testing facilities for LPG Cylinder 
as per IS 3196 Part 1:2013 and Domestic Electrical 
Food Mixer (Liquidizers & Grinders) and Centrifugal 
Juicers as per IS 4250:1980. BNBOL (Bangalore 
Branch Office Laboratory) has created complete 
testing facilities for pressure cooker as per IS 2347: 
2017. WROL(Western Regional Office Laboratory), 
Mumbai has enhanced the testing capacity for 
Switches ( IS 3854: 1997)  and Sockets( IS 1293:2019) 
and Cables ( IS 694: 2010 & IS 1554 (Part 1 ) 1988) 
through procurement of additional test equipment 
thereby reducing the turnaround time of reporting.
Quality Assurance Activities
Quality Assurance is a regular part of testing in BIS 
Labs by way of which the quality of testing is assured. 
650 samples were tested under Quality Assurance 
Activities by BIS Labs in the year 2019-20. These also 
include the samples tested during participation in a 
proficiency testing and inter-lab comparison program.
Lab Recognition Scheme
BIS operates a Laboratory Recognition Scheme (LRS) 
for recognition of Outside Laboratories (OSLs). The 
scheme is based on International Standard IS/ISO/
IEC 17025, and in line with the criteria adopted by the 
NABL for accreditation of laboratories. During 2019-
2020, 13 new labs were recognized and five labs were 
de-recognized. As on 31 March 2020, the total 
number of operative BIS recognized labs were 246, 
which include reputed R&D organizations, technical 
institutions, Government labs and private sector labs 
(77 Government labs and 169 Private labs). Besides 
this, services of 255 Government laboratories of 
specialized nature are utilized for different products, 
as and when required. To support BIS Compulsory 
Registration Scheme, 65 outside labs are testing IT 
products, which were covered under Electronics and 
Information Technology Goods (Requirement for 
Compulsory Registration) Order, 2012.
Testing Facility for Hallmarking Activity
Referral Assaying laboratories for Gold testing play 
an important role in the Hallmarking Scheme by 
testing the samples of hallmarked gold and acting 
as a check on the assaying centre testing activities. 
Referral assay laboratories for Gold testing were 
functional earlier at SROL Chennai and Central 
Laboratory, Sahibabad. Referral Assaying Laboratory 
was established at Eastern Regional Laboratory 
(EROL), Kolkata during 2019-20 and the same was 
inaugurated by DG,  BIS on 20 August 2019. The 
laboratory will cater to the requirements for referral 
assay testing for entire Eastern and North Eastern 
India.
LABORATORY SERVICES

Launch of National Laboratory Directory by Hon’ble Minister of CA, F and PD
National Lab Directory
BIS created a National Laboratory Directory 
which was launched by Shri Ram Vilas 
Paswan, Hon’ble Minister of Consumer 
Affairs, Food and Public Distribution on 
30 August 2019. National Lab Directory 
is an online platform created on the basis 
of self-entry by testing laboratories about 
the available testing facilities in their labs. 
The directory aims to bring together the 
information about test facilities available in 
the country on a single online platform for 
use by various stakeholders. Any Laboratory 
across the country can register on the portal, 
free of cost and enter the details of the testing 
facilities available with them. The consumers 
on the other side get a platform where they 
can search for laboratories available in the 
country for testing of any product.
Drinking Water Quality Testing
In keeping with the objectives of the Jal 
Jeevan Mission of Hon’ble Prime Minister, 
BIS, as per the directions of Ministry of 
Consumer Affairs Food & PD, undertook a 
study of the quality of piped drinking water 
being supplied in the country.
In the first phase, samples of drinking water 
were drawn from various locations across 
Delhi, and in the second phase, samples 
were drawn from 20 other state capitals. The 
samples of piped water being supplied by city 
municipalities/ Corporation/ Water Board/
local bodies were drawn from different 
locations at consumer’s end and sent for 
testing as per IS 10500:2012 (Specification 
for Drinking Water) at NABL accredited labs 
across India. The city wise test results were 
published on BIS website. In the third phase, 
samples from the state capital cities of North 
Eastern States and Smart Cities identified by 
the Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs 
have been drawn and the analysis of results 
has been submitted to Ministry of Consumer 
Affairs.
Workshop on Standard for Drinking Water
A One Day Workshop on ‘Ensuring Quality 
of Piped Drinking Water’ was organized 
by BIS on 09 December 2019 with an 
aim to disseminate the information about 
the requirements of IS 10500:2012 and 
other Indian Standards. The workshop was 
inaugurated by Shri Ram Vilas Paswan, 
Union Minister for Consumer Affairs Food 
& Public Distribution and was attended 
by participants 
from 23 States/Union 
territories representing their respective 
Water Supply and PHE Departments.  During 
the workshop, information regarding the 
certification schemes and testing facilities 
required for compliance with IS 10500:2012 
for providing safe drinking water was also 
disseminated.
The 
similarities 
between 
relevant 
International Standards, Indian Standard 
and WHO Guidelines were also explained. 
Representatives from various states updated 
about the measures being taken to ensure 
supply of safe drinking water and suggestions 
regarding same were also discussed.
New Initiatives
During the period April 2019 – Mar 2020, 
number of hallmarking registrations has grown 
from 26688 to 30626 while the number of BIS 
recognized Assaying and Hallmarking centres 
have increased from 797 to 915. During the same 
period, 3.89 crore articles of gold and silver 
jewellery/ artefacts have been hallmarked.
Hallmarking Scheme of Gold/Silver Jewellery / 
artefact
Hallmarking of gold jewellery was started by BIS 
in April 2000 to provide third party assurance to 
consumers on the purity of gold jewellery or its 
fineness. The scheme for Hallmarking of silver 
jewellery/ artefacts was launched in October 
2005. Under the Scheme, while the jewellers are 
granted registration to sell hallmarked jewellery, 
Assaying & Hallmarking centres are recognized 
to assay the purity of the jewellery submitted by 
the registered jeweller, along with declaration of 
purity and to apply hallmark on such jewellery 
which is found conforming to relevant Indian 
Standard including declared fineness.
Mandatory Hallmarking
Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Government of 
India has issued an order on 15 January 2020 for 
making hallmarking of gold jewellery mandatory 
as per provisions given in BIS Act, 2016. This 
order makes it compulsory for all the jewellers 
selling Gold jewellery and artefacts to register 
with BIS and sell Hallmarked gold jewellery 
and artefacts of 14, 18 and 22 carat only w.e.f 
15 January 2021.  
Hallmarking of Gold Bullion
Hallmarking of gold Bullion in the fineness of 
999 & 995 as per IS 1417: 2016 was started in 
October 2015. Under this scheme the licence 
is granted to refineries/mint which carry out 
refining of gold by electrolytic or aquaregia 
process and have laboratory with complete test 
facility and accredited by NABL. 
29 licences have been granted to refineries/ India 
Government Mint for Gold bullion and coin as on 
31 March 2020.  
Gold Monetization Scheme
Govt. of India has launched Gold Monetization 
Scheme on 05 November 2015. BIS has played an 
important role in finalization and implementation 
of the Gold Monetization Scheme in association 
with Department of Economic Affairs and 
Reserve Bank of India. Under the scheme 
Assaying & Hallmarking Centres recognized by 
BIS have been qualified to act as Collection and 
Purity Testing Centres (CPTC). 
So far 48 A&H centres and one Jeweller have 
been qualified to act as CPTC. The gold collected 
by CPTCs is to be refined by refineries licensed by 
BIS.
Promotion of Hallmarking
To promote hallmarking in the country for 
effective consumer protection in gold jewellery 
trade, awareness programmes for jewellers are 
organized by BIS through its various Regional 
and Branch offices across the country. During the 
year, 78 such jewellers’ awareness programmes 
were organized.

INTERNATIONAL ACTIVITIES
BIS in its capacity as the National Standards 
Body of India, is a member of International 
Organization for Standardization (ISO) and 
through the Indian National Committee a 
member of International Electrotechnical 
Commission (IEC) and actively participates 
in the activities of these International bodies. 
BIS is involved in development of International 
Standards as Participating (P) members or 
Observer (O) members on various Technical 
Committees and Sub-Committees; and has 
nominated technical experts in Working 
Groups of these committees. 
As on 31 March 2020; BIS is a ‘P’ member in 
470 Technical Committees/ Subcommittees 
of ISO and - 96 Technical Committees/ 
Subcommittees of IEC, and ‘O’ Member in 197 
Technical Committees/ Subcommittees of ISO 
and 74 Technical Committees/ Subcommittees 
of IEC. BIS holds the Secretariat of 10 important 
ISO Committees dealing with subjects that 
are of interest to India. Chairmanship of three 
IEC Technical Committee/ Subcommittee/ 
Systems Committee and vice chair of one 
Systems Committee are also held by India. 
BIS also participates in various policy-making 
committees of these international standards 
bodies, having ‘P’ membership in three 
Policy Development Committees (CASCO, 
COPOLCO and DEVCO) of ISO. India was a 
member in ISO Council, represented by DG 
BIS, the apex policy committee of ISO (Term 
2017-19) and a member of ISO Council 
Standing Committee on Finance (CSC/FIN) 
and ISO Council Standing Committee on 
Oversight (CSC/OVE) for term 2018-19 till 
31 December 2019. DG BIS is convener of 
the ISO DEVCO/ WG 2 “Resources required 
for enhance NSB capacities in standard 
development and use”. India is also a member 
in Standards Management Board (SMB) of 
IEC. BIS has successfully contested for ISO 
Technical Management Board (TMB), the apex 
body of ISO for technical issues and is currently 
a member of TMB for the term 2020-2022 
starting from 01 January 2020. 
Convenorship of  22 ISO working groups and 
co-convenorship of one ISO working group 
is presently with India, out of which that of 
ISO/IEC JTC 1/SC 7/AG 1 “Chair’s Advisory 
Group”, ISO/IEC JTC 1/SC 7/AG 2 “Business 
Planning Group”, ISO/IEC JTC 1/SC 7/AG 3 
“Communications and outreach”, ISO/IEC JTC 
1/SC 7/AG 5 “Architecture and future watch”, 
ISO/TC 34/SC 8/WG 12 “Instant tea in solid 
form”, ISO/PC 316/SG 2 “Lavatory and urinal 
equipment”, ISO/TC 307/AHG 2 “Guidance for 
auditing DLT Systems” and ISO/TC 309/AHG 
4 “Governance Maturity Model” were taken 
up during this period. Similarly, India holds 
convenorship of eight working groups/ adhoc 
group/ task groups/ management team of IEC 
out of which that of IEC Systems Committee 
on Smarts Cities working group 1 (IEC/ SyC 
Smart Cities/ WG 1) “Terminology” was taken 
up during this period.
Members of technical committees and officers 
of BIS participated in various ISO/IEC Technical 
Committees and contributed effectively in 
the development of various International 
Standards. BIS has submitted a proposal for 
establishment of a new field of activity by 
establishing a new technical committee in 
ISO on “Security equipments for financial 
institutions and commercial organizations”. 
The proposal is currently under member body 
consultation through a 12-week member body 
ballot starting from 28 February 2020.  During 
this period, India proposed new work items 
related to instant tea in solid forms, Health 
Informatics- Introduction to Ayurveda, etc. 
During the year, 49 delegations participated in 
ISO/ IEC technical committee meetings.
BIS has also been significantly contributing 
in the formulation of regional standards for 
the SAARC countries under the South Asian 
Regional Standards Organization (SARSO) and 
in the work of Pacific Area Standards Congress 
(PASC).

prestigious

World Manufacturer Identifier (WMI) Number
In co-ordination with the Society of Automotive 
Engineers (SAE), USA; BIS issues WMI Codes

Manufacturer 
Identifier 
(WMI) 
Code’ 
to 
automobile manufacturers and exporters in India. 
204 WMI Codes were allotted to various vehicle 
manufacturers during the period. 
Registered Application Provider Identifier (RID) 
Code
It is a part of an Application Identifier (AID), which 
is structured and assigned according to ISO/
IEC 7816-5 and consists of two parts - RID and 
Proprietary Application Identifier Extension (PIX) 
used to identify different applications or products. 
In a multi-application environment, an application 
provider needs to be able to uniquely identify itself 
and its application in the card and the terminal 
needs to be able to support the application. In 
case application is to be used internationally, one 
needs to apply for an international RID. Each 
Application Provider is assigned one unique RID. 
The Application Provider uses the PIX to identify its 
different applications or products. One application 
for RID was processed during the period.
Technical Information 
Services
Research is the cornerstone of standardization 
process. Research produces knowledge that 
is distilled and updated during the process of 
standards formulation for making it suitable for 
application outside a research setting. Research 
provides structured methods and reliable data 
that aid evidence-based decision making, and also 
forms the foundation of policy and programme 
development. With a view to put in place such 
vibrant eco- system of research and dynamic policy 
formulation as also for orienting the capacity 
building programme to the changing need of a 
dynamic policy regime, a new department, namely 
‘Policy, Research and Training Department’ has 
been created in BIS. 
Research Advisory Committee
To guide the research activities of the Bureau, 
a Research Advisory Committee has been 
constituted. 
The 
Committee, 
under 
the 
Chairpersonship of Director General, BIS will 
have Members from key research organizations in 
the country and Deputy Director General (Policy, 
Research and Training) will be the Member 
Secretary. The Committee shall advise on:
	
Policy Matters relating to research activities 
on standardization, conformity assessment 
and other related activities; 
	
Collaboration with institutions/organizations 
within the country and abroad on matters 
relating to research;  
	
Promoting and planning research including 
evaluation/impact assessment studies and 
surveys; and 
	
Other matters relating to research.
Action Research by Employees of the Bureau
BIS has initiated action w.r.t. seeking Action 
Research Project proposals from Scientific 
Cadre Officers, to foster a vibrant atmosphere of 
creativity, innovation and research in the Bureau, 
where fertile minds can work on generation 
of knowledge. The Officers have responded 
enthusiastically to this initiative by identifying 
and submitting proposals for Action Research 
covering a wide spectrum of areas and activities of 
BIS. Till 31 March 2020, a total of 270 proposals 
have been submitted by the Officers.

BIS endeavours to provide its services and the 
benefits of standardization and certification 
to all its stakeholders in a focused manner. 
Think, Nudge and Move Department of BIS 
spearheads the communication strategy of BIS 
and engagement with its diverse stakeholders. 
This department of BIS handles various 
consumer related activities which include 
redressal of public grievances, organizing 
various awareness programmes, interaction 
with stakeholders of BIS, consumer protection, 
holding of World Standards Day and publicity 
related activities. 
Consumer Awareness Programmes 
For promoting the concept of standardization, 
certification and quality consciousness among 
consumers, 199 awareness programmes were 
organized on a regular basis through various 
Regional and Branch Offices. during the year. 
Industry Awareness Programmes 
To propagate the concept of standardization, 
product certification, management systems 
certification and other BIS activities amongst 
Industries, 69 Industry awareness programmes 
were organized during the year. 
Educational 
Utilization 
of 
Standards 
Programmes 
BIS organizes Educational Utilization of 
Standards Programmes (EUS) for students and 
teachers of schools, colleges, etc, to inculcate 
the young minds with the concepts and benefits 
of standardization. During the year, 27 EUS 
Programmes were organized.
World Standards Day
BIS celebrates the World Standards Day by 
arranging Seminars/Conferences across the 
country at its Regional offices and Branch 
offices on theme identified by International 
Organization 
for 
Standardization 
(ISO), 
International 
Electrotechnical 
Commission 
(IEC) and International Tele-communication 
Union (ITU). The theme for the year 2019 was 
“Video Standards Create a Global Stage”
On the occasion of World Standards Day, 
seminar at the national level was held at 
New Delhi on 14 October 2019. The event 
was inaugurated by Shri Ram Vilas Paswan, 
Hon’ble Minister (Consumer Affairs, Food and 
Public Distribution), Govt. of India.  
CONSUMER  AFFAIRS  &  PUBLICITY
BIS sells Indian Standards (IS) and Special 
Publications (SP) through 25 different sales 
outlets located at the Headquarters (HQs), 
Regional and Branch Offices. Sale is also done 
through registered booksellers spread across 
major cities of India.  BIS also sells foreign 
standards (ISO, IEC, BSI London, DIN Germany, 
JIS Japan) in India from BIS Headquarters 
(HQs).  In addition, Indian Standards and 
Special Publications are also sold through 
e-portal 
(www.standardsbis.bsbedge.com). 
Indian Standards are now available free of cost 
in downloadable version for non-commercial 
purposes through e-portal.  Alternatively, an 
order for hard copy can be placed through the 
e-portal. Online payment can be made over 
the portal through credit/debit card.  Facility 
is available to make payment for purchase of 
Indian Standards through NEFT/RTGS (online 
transfer). 
The Indian Standards are also available 
as a complete set in DVD or 14 different 
department/ sector specific sets like civil 
engineering, electrical engineering, mechanical 
engineering, textiles, etc. on subscription basis. 
In addition, Indian Standards were also made 
available through online subscription. National 
Building Code (NBC) 2016 is also available on 
DVD/online subscription.  
For education & research sector - BIS has 
further made available Indian Standards on 
DVD/online subscription basis to educational 
institutions, 
scientific 
and 
research 
laboratories, Central and State Government 
Laboratories/Research Institution operating 
through consortia.
For corporate / enterprises sector - BIS has also 
made available Indian Standards on DVD/online 
subscription which includes access of Indian 
Standards through WAN (Wide Area Network) 
for a single corporate entity operating from 
multi-locations.
For the ease of customers, connection to BIS 
e-portal 
(www.standardsbis.bsbedge.com) 
provided at the Sales Department of BIS HQ, 
the customers can search the standard of their 
requirement, see the price of standards, scope, 
preview of Standards, amendments issued, etc. 
before purchase of the same.
SALE  OF STANDARDS  
AND  OTHER 
PUBLICATIONS
Public Grievances 
BIS follows a well-established complaint 
redressal procedure. Consumers can lodge 
their grievances relating to BIS certified 
products through offline and online methods. 
Complaints 
are 
monitored 
regularly 
for 
redressal. During the year, 203 complaints 
related to quality of BIS Standard marked 
products and unauthorized use of BIS Standard 
mark were received. 104 cases were remaining 
pending at the end of the year 2018-19 due to 
processing at various stages. Out of the above, 
143 complaints were redressed during this 
period.
Enforcement 
The BIS Standard Mark (ISI Mark) is a mark of 
quality. During 2019-20, BIS carried out 104 
Search & Seizures all over the country on firms 
misusing ISI Mark and such products were 
seized. Efforts were made for timely launching 
of prosecution against the offenders in the 
Court of law. BIS also issued press releases 
regarding the enforcement raids for giving 
wide publicity with the intention to create 
awareness among the consumers about the 
unscrupulous manufacturers misusing the ISI 
mark. The details of the raids and judgements 
are hosted on the BIS website.
Publicity 
BIS creates awareness about its various 
activities through print, electronic, outdoor and 
digital media. In 2019-20, publicity campaigns 
were executed mainly to promote importance 
of ISI-Mark, Hallmark on gold jewellery and 
compulsory registration scheme. BIS is also 
active on all popular social media platforms 
and posts are regularly uploaded regarding 
importance of Standards, other key activities 
of BIS and events. The total budget allocated in 
2019-20 for publicity activities is Rs. 20 crores 
which was further allocated as Rs 60 lakh to 
each of the Regional offices and Rs 5 lakhs to 
each of the Branch Offices for reaching the 
target audience on Pan India level.

In line with the Government of India’s vision 
of Digital India and Ease of doing business, BIS 
is actively progressing towards automating 
all its activities through development and 
implementation of software applications/online 
portals under e-BIS project. It is making all efforts 
to render software solutions/online portals 
user-friendly and creating a platform for ease 
of doing business for its various stakeholders. 
With this vision in mind BIS has taken following 
major steps towards ease of doing business for its 
stakeholders during the FY 2019-20:
Minister’s Dashboard
As per the directions of Shri Ram Vilas Paswan, 
Hon’ble Minister for Consumer Affairs, Food & 
Public Distribution, GoI, a Minister’s dashboard 
for displaying the details of BIS activities has been 
made operational. 
Standards Management Software
A software is in operation to manage the Standard 
Formulation activity of BIS which automates all 
the stages involved in the formulation of standards 
and their amendments. It enables outside users 
to easily search relevant Indian Standards, to 
submit their comments on any published Indian 
Standard or document in wide circulation online. 
This software has been enhanced to generate 
management 
control 
reports 
for 
effective 
monitoring. Portals are also operational for online 
sale and subscription of Indian Standards. 
BIS-Connect software
This software has been made operational to 
facilitate information dissemination, sharing 
of 
documents 
and 
data 
with 
concerned 
stakeholders, etc. The software has various 
features like document repository, notice board, 
drafts circulation, ballot, comment register, 
new proposal submission, Indian Standard 
locator, feedback etc which provide customized 
dashboards, notifications and update to users. 
Web Portal for Conformity Assessment 
Schemes of BIS
A web portal is in operation to cater to product 
certification scheme, related laboratory activities 
and management system certification of BIS. This 
portal enables applicants/licencees to apply for a 
new licence, renewal, inclusion, payment of fees, 
etc., online for grant of licence to use standard 
mark. Subsequently, BIS can process these 
requests and communicate their decision online. 
This portal has been enhanced to make it more 
efficient and user friendly.
CRSBIS
This portal is in operation to manage grant of 
registrations online to Electronics & IT Goods 
notified 
under 
compulsory 
registration 
by 
MeitY. It has online features like submission of 
applications by manufacturers, scrutiny by BIS, 
grant of registration, inclusion of models, renewals 
and other monitoring/operational aspects. This 
portal is upgraded time to time in consultation 
with the user departments and feedback received 
from stakeholders. 
Human Resource and Finance Management 
System (HFMS)
This system was procured for managing the 
various support services of BIS such as Human 
Resource, Finance, Accounts, Administration, 
Establishment, 
etc. 
Various 
modules 
for 
generation of salary & pension, service book of 
employees, annual performance appraisal reports 
for scientific cadre and inventory management 
system have been made operational. The other 
features of the HFMS viz. annual immovable 
property return, budget, cash, third party 
payments, compilation, transfer & promotion are 
being customized.
World Trade Organization-Technical Barriers 
to Trade (WTO-TBT) 
A module is operational to host WTO-TBT 
notifications. It enables outside users to register 
themselves to receive notifications on their email 
IDs. This module has been enhanced to make it 
more user-friendly.
Mobile App
An android based mobile app has been made 
operational for Self-Declaration of Conformity 
(SDOC). This fast, simple and user friendly app 
enables user to fetch details of a registered 
manufacturer based on R-no. The user can also 
scan QR-code provided on Licence document 
issued by BIS to a manufacturer.
Pashmina micro-site
A micro-site for disseminating information 
regarding hallmarking of pashmina has been made 
operational during the adventure rally organized 
in July-August 2019.
Wi-Fi
Both the buildings of BIS-HQ have been Wi-Fi 
enabled w.e.f.  01 October 2019.
The bandwidth in BIS-HQ and BIS offices is being 
upgraded to 1 Gbps and 16 Mbps respectively. 
The work of commissioning the Optical Fiber 
Cables (OFC) in BIS offices has been completed.
INFORMATION  TECHNOLOGY SERVICES
Server Room
A new server room has been created in BIS-HQ to 
house servers and net-working equipments with 
adequate cooling and fire detection facility. 
Further, in order to make our applications cater to 
new user requirements, emerging technologies 
(e.g. Artificial Intelligence, Data Analytics) and 
user-friendly & efficient systems, following 
initiatives are also being taken:
	
A new comprehensive software (e-BIS) has 
been envisaged encompassing all activities 
of BIS and with advanced features like 
data analytics, artificial intelligence, good 
MIS for effective monitoring, user-friendly 
interfaces, etc. The e-BIS will provide an 
integrated platform for all stakeholders- 
Industry, Consumers, Labs, BIS, etc. Various 
Discussion Foras and Recommendation 
Forums have been constituted for timely 
finalization of requirements. 
	
An online portal is being developed for 
recognition of SDOs in India and their 
standards as Indian Standards. Registration 
screens of SDOs have been developed. 
Application 
submission 
module 
has 
also 
been 
developed & is under testing.
	
The integrated web portal 
is being enhanced to cater 
to the foreign manufacturer 
certification, 
hallmarking 
and 
related 
consumer 
affairs 
activities 
of 
BIS. 
These modules have been 
developed 
& 
are 
under 
testing.
	
National Informatics Centre’s 
e-Office Lite software is being 
implemented in BIS. Training of employees 
for effective utilization of the software is 
being initiated. 
	
A web portal is being developed to enable 
stakeholders to raise technical queries 
related to activities of BIS. The portal 
facilitates various departments of BIS to 
view & reply to these queries along with 
attachments. 
	
A portal is being developed to facilitate 
automotive manufacturers to obtain World 
Manufacturer Identifier (WMI) code online 
from BIS, which is the WMI coordinator in 
India. 
	
A new portal is being developed to cater to all 
the training requirements of BIS. This portal 
would automate entire process involved 
from the inception of a training programme 
till its completion. It has features like viewing 
of trainings programmes in training calendar, 
planning of training programme, receiving 
of online applications, hostel booking, 
online payments, faculties management, 
programme creations, etc.

VIGILANCE ACTIVITIES
Vigilance Awareness Week 2019 - Award Ceremony
The vigilance Set up of Bureau of Indian 
Standards is headed by the Chief Vigilance 
Officer 
and 
comprises 
of 
Vigilance 
Department at BIS Headquarters and a 
Vigilance Section in the Secretariat of each 
of the Disciplinary Authority for Group B 
& C employees (Deputy Director General 
concerned).
The Vigilance Department functions in 
close coordination with Central Vigilance 
Commission(CVC), 
Central 
Bureau 
of Investigation(CBI) and Ministry of 
Consumer Affairs, Food & Public Distribution. 
It is entrusted with the responsibility of 
managing all vigilance related activities of 
the Bureau in accordance with the guidelines 
on the subject issued by Central Vigilance 
Commission, DoPT etc. This, inter-alia, 
include activities related to:
	
Preventive vigilance (e.g. streamlining 
of procedures, training, preparation 
of ‘Agreed List’ and ‘List of officers of 
doubtful integrity’ etc.)
	
Punitive Vigilance (e.g. scrutiny of 
complaints 
received, 
investigations, 
disciplinary action against the officers at 
fault etc.)
	
Surveillance and detection (e.g. Vigilance 
Audits, scrutiny of annual property 
returns, monitoring, review meetings 
etc.)
Vigilance Awareness Week was celebrated 
in Bureau of Indian Standards from 28 
October 2019 to 02 November 2019. 
During the week, a number of activities, 
including display of banners & distribution of 
pamphlets, Quiz Competition, Essay Writing 
Competition, Slogan Writing Competition 
and Painting Competition, on the theme 
“Integrity- A Way of Life” were organized. 
Workshops were held on CCS- CCA Rules; 
Leave Rules; Medical Rules and General 
Financial Rules (GFR), 2017. A magazine 
“Manak Darpan” was also published.
Preventive vigilance audits of Bhopal Branch 
Office, Pune Branch Office, and Nagpur 
Branch Office were carried out during the 
year.
Workshops on Preventive Vigilance was 
organized in Western Regional Office, 
Mumbai and Bhopal Branch Office.
A Workshop was held on 02 March 2020 
on Awareness and Implementation of IS/
ISO 37001:2016 Anti Bribery Management 
System in BIS. During the workshop, 
participants 
were 
informed 
regarding 
various facets of IS/ISO 37001:2016 – Anti 
Bribery Management System including Risk 
Assessment.

LIBRARY SERVICES
Located at headquarters, the BIS Technical 
Library is a National Resource Centre for 
information on standards and related matters 
and serves industry, trade, government, 
researchers and consumers. It is the largest 
library of Standards in the South Asian 
Region, with a floor area of 1,000 square 
meters. The collection includes about 4 lakh 
Standards from world and approximate 
73,082 technical books. From 01 April 
2019 to 31 March 2020, 2175 visitors were 
provided with reference material of their 
need. It assisted  the Trade and Industry 
by answering 1868 queries. The Library 
regularly updates mechanized database 
of publications (books and standards) 
received. Under the Annual Action Plan, 
61197 numbers of publications have been 
electronically indexed from 01 April 2019 to 
31 March 2020. 
SEXUAL HARASSMENT OF WORKING 
WOMEN AT WORKPLACE
In compliance with the guidelines of the 
Supreme Court of India on the prevention of 
sexual harassment of women at work place, 
and later on under POSH Act, an Internal 
Complaints Committee was constituted in 
Bureau of Indian Standards in Feb 1998 that 
has since been reconstituted in January 2020. 
The Composition of the newly constituted 
Internal Complaints Committee (ICC) at 
BIS, HQ- New Delhi consists of a senior 
level woman employee as Presiding Officer 
along with two other members from BIS-
HQ and one of the members from All India 
Democratic Women’s Association (AIDWA), 
New Delhi. 
The ICC is proactively functioning in BIS 
under the POSH Act.
SCHEMES 
FOR 
PERSONS 
WITH 
DISABILITIES
	
As per the GoI instructions, 4% 
reservation is being provided to the 
persons 
with 
disabilities 
in 
direct 
recruitment under Group A, B & C posts. 
  	 BIS also allows 12 casual leaves to the 
persons with disabilities instead of 08 
Casual leaves permissible to others.
 	
Persons with disabilities are being 
paid Transport Allowance at double 
the normal prescribed rate for others, 
subject to a minimum of Rs. 2250/- per 
month.
RESERVATION 
FOR 
ECONOMICALLY 
WEAKER SECTIONS (EWSs) IN DIRECT 
RECRUITMENT
 	
As per the GoI instructions, 10% 
reservation is being provided to the 
Economically Weaker Sections (EWSs) 
in Direct Recruitment.
OUR FINANCE
For the 31st consecutive year i.e. 2019-20, Bureau of Indian Standards continued to be 
self-reliant in meeting its expenditure and other liabilities. The total income (excluding 
income from investment) during the year 2019-20 amounted to `77023.69 lakh as 
against `70579.52 lakh in the previous year resulting in an increase of 9.13%. The largest 
contribution to the income was from Product Certification Marking Fee which stood at 
`61677.06 lakh against `55410.11 lakh in the previous year i.e. an increase of 11.31%. The 
total revenue expenditure during the year 2019-20 amounted to `28573.21 lakh as against 
`27901.09 lakh in 2018-19 registering an increase of 2.41%.

SCHEDULE FORMING PART OF THE ACCOUNTS FOR  
THE PERIOD ENDED 31 MARCH 2020
SCHEDULE 16 
SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES
1.	
ACCCOUNTING CONVENTION
	
The Financial Statements are prepared on the basis of historical cost convention, 
unless otherwise stated and generally on the accrual method of accounting except the 
Income from Services and the interest due on default investments which are accounted 
on cash basis.
2.	
INVENTORIES
	
The value of Stock of Indian Standards and other publications are not accounted for 
as a matter of policy.  However, the Stock of Paper, Laboratory Consumables, Spares, 
Stationery and Gold are valued at cost.
3.	
INVESTMENT
3.1	
The investments are usually carried at cost.  
3.2	
The premium paid on acquisition of permanent investment is amortized on a time 
proportion basis upto the date of maturity. 
4.	
FIXED ASSET
4.1	
Fixed Assets are stated at Cost of Acquisition inclusive of Inward Freight, Duties and 
Taxes.
4.2	
Fixed Assets acquired out of Grants/Assistance from Ministries are capitalized at 
values stated by corresponding credit to Corpus/ Capital Fund.
4.3	
Fixed Assets received by way of non-monetary grants are capitalized at values stated 
by corresponding credit to Corpus/Capital Fund.
5.	
DEPRECIATION
	
Depreciation is provided on written down value method as per the rates specified in 
the Income Tax Act 1961.
6.	
GOVERNMENT GRANTS/ASSISTANCE
6.1	
Government Grants/Assistance are accounted on realization basis.
6.2	
All Government Grants/Assistance from Ministries and their utilization are shown in 
the Earmarked/Endowment Fund Schedule.    
6.3	
The Government Grants/Assistance utilized towards Capital Cost of setting of projects 
and acquisition of Fixed Asset are shown as addition to Corpus/Capital Fund.
7.	
FOREIGN CURRENCY TRANSACTIONS 
7.1	
Transactions denominated in Foreign Currency are accounted at the exchange rate 
prevailing at the date of the transaction.
7.2	
Current Liabilities are converted at the exchange rate prevailing as at the end of the 
year and the relevant gain/loss is transferred to Income & Expenditure Account.
8.	
PAY & ALLOWANCES 
	
The payments of Pay & Allowances and Leave Encashment are charged to Income & 
Expenditure Account on cash basis under Pay and Allowances.
9.	
RETIREMENT BENEFITS
9.1	
Liability towards pension of retired employees and pension & gratuity of existing 
employees for past service based on the Actuarial Valuation is accrued and provided in 
the Pension & Gratuity Liability Fund Account shown under the Schedule - Earmarked/
Endowment Fund.  
9.2	
Based on the Actuarial Valuation Report, contribution towards shortfall in the fund is 
charged to the Income & Expenditure Account with corresponding credit to Pension & 
Gratuity Liability Fund Account.  
9.3	
The actual payments of all pensionary benefits and recurring pension during the year 
are debited to Pension & Gratuity Liability Fund Account. 
10.	
LOANS TO EMPLOYEES
	
The Interest on House Building, Conveyance and Computer Loan given to employees 
is accounted on cash basis after the recovery of the principal amount of Loan.
11. 	
GPF ACCOUNTS
	
The surplus/deficit in the GPF Account of employees are treated as income/expense of 
the  Bureau.

2.2 	
Pension & Gratuity Liability Fund Account (Schedule 2-column 7)
2.2.1 The Rule 44 of BIS Rules, 2018 provides that BIS shall maintain a Pension & Gratuity 
Liability Fund which shall be utilized for the payment of pension, gratuity and 
commuted value of pension. The corpus of the fund shall be on the basis of actuarial 
valuation and shortfall, if any, shall be made through the appropriation of the surplus 
from income and expenditure account.
2.2.2	 Actuarial Valuation of Pension & Gratuity Liability of BIS was carried out by M/s. Nalin 
Kapadia, Consultant & Actuaries, registered with SEBI by following the guidelines 
given in AS-15 of Institute of Chartered Accountants of India and the guidelines of 
the Actuaries Society of India and the total accrued Pension & Gratuity Liability of BIS 
as per the report submitted by them works out to `2016.07 crore as on 31.03.2020 
which is given as under:

2.3	
National Pension Scheme Fund: The National Pension Scheme(NPS) is applicable to 
all employees who joined BIS after 01.01.2004. The employees’ contribution and BIS 
contribution in respect of those who are enrolled with the Regulator are remitted to 
PFRDA on monthly basis. However, the employees contribution and BIS contribution 
in respect of employees who are yet to be enrolled with the Regulator is kept with BIS 
under NPS Fund Account and is invested by BIS. The interest calculated at the rate 
equivalent to the interest rate of GPF is credited to their accounts. The balance in the 
NPS Fund with BIS as on 31.03.2020 amounted to `31,41,000 {Schedule 2, column 6}.

2.4.2	 BIS had made investment of `200.00 lakh in the bonds of U.P. Cooperative & 
Spinning Mills Federation Ltd. (UPCSMFL), an undertaking of Uttar Pradesh 
Government on 17.12.1998 @ 16% per annum. UPCSMFL had defaulted in the 
payment of interest and principal on maturity dates. The maturity of principal was 
due on 30.04.2003(33%), 30.10.2003(33%) and 30.04.2004(34%). The interest is 
under default since 01.05.2000 which amounts to `128.00 lakh till date of maturity 
at coupon rate. BIS had filed a case through Petition No. 451/2002 before the 
Hon’ble National Consumer Disputes Redressal Commission (NCDRC). The Hon’ble 
NCDRC pronounced its judgement on 01.02.2016 and ordered the opposite party 
No. 01(UPCSMFL) & opposite party No. 02(Government of Uttar Pradesh, the 
guarantor) to pay a sum of `200 lakh jointly and severally to BIS alongwith the 
interest @ 9% only from 01.05.2000 till the date of realization. A Caveat Petition has 
also been filed by BIS in the Hon’ble Supreme Court on 03.03.2016.
	
The UPCSMFL filed the Review Petitions No. 48/2017 & 138/2017 praying the Hon’ble 
NCDRC to (i) allow this Review Petition and recall order dated 01 February 2016 
passed by this Hon’ble Court in Original Petition No. 451/2002, and (ii) set aside order 
01 February 2016 passed by this Hon’ble Court in Original Petition No. 451/2002 and 
hear this case afresh. The Review Petitions were dismissed by Hon’ble NCDRC. The 
Hon’ble NCDRC has issued Dasti Notice of E.A. No. 43 of 2016 and of all M.A.s filed 
therein to the Respondents/Judgement Debtors and to the Official Liquidator.
	
The case was listed on 04 July 2019 for appearance of Opposite Parties. However, 
fresh Dasti Notice of the Execution Petition No. 43 of 2016 and all Miscellaneous 
Applications filed therein, were issued to the Opposite Parties (Judgement Debtors 
and the Official Liquidators of Opposite Party No.1) returnable on 16.09.2019 because 
of the non-service of previous notice.
	
Subsequently, the case was listed again on 16.09.2019, 27.11.2019 and 06.03.2020 
and Hon’ble Commission directed the Bureau to file the information as to where and 
before which Court the liquidation proceedings are currently pending, including the 
procedure for execution of a decree when the matter is under liquidation. Since none 
was present on behalf of Official Liquidator on 06.03.2020, Hon’ble Commission 
adjourned the matter to 09.07.2020.

2.7	
Consumer Awareness & Protection Fund: A fund namely “Consumer Awareness 
& Protection Fund” has been created by BIS and all the fines and penalties received 
have been credited to this fund. As per Section 20 of BIS Act, 2016, the fines received 
shall be used for consumer awareness, consumer protection & promotion of quality 
of goods, articles, processes, systems or services in the country. Accordingly, the 
expenses incurred during the year towards consumer awareness programmes have 
been debited to the fund to the extent of the balance of `33,97,235 available in the 
fund.
2.8	
New Central AC Plant for Manak Bhawan Building by NBCC: The project of 
installation of New Central AC Plant for Manak Bhawan at HQ was initiated in the 
year 2003-04. National Building Construction Corporation (NBCC) was appointed as 
Project Management Consultant(PMC) for the project. The payment of `86.07 lakh 
was made to NBCC. However, the project was stopped in June 2006. The settlement 
of account could not be made due to dispute regarding the manner of calculation of 
consultancy charges payable to NBCC. It was, therefore, decided that no payment 
shall be released to NBCC against other projects namely Construction of JBO Building 
and NITS Noida till the settlement of accounts with NBCC towards this project. The 
payments of `84,38,368 made upto 2008-09 under this project have been shown 
as Capital work-in-progress in the Schedule of Fixed Assets[Schedule 4]. Executive 
Committee(EC) in its 79th meeting held on 27 March 2008 had decided to close the 
contract and agreement with NBCC and also approved the project related to air 
conditioning of both Manak Bhawan & Manakalaya, and related civil and electrical 
works to be undertaken through CPWD. This project by CPWD has been executed 
and the AC plant is in operation.
2.9	
Subscription to ISO/IEC: The C&AG in its Separate Audit Report for the year 2018-19 
had given an observation that BIS has been accounting subscription for membership 
of International Organization for Standardization (ISO), International Electrotechnical 
Commission (IEC) on cash basis instead of accrual basis in contravention of its declared 
accounting policy.
	
Therefore, the existing accounting practice with respect of the above expenditure has 
been changed from the current year. As a result of this, the expenditure of `1,44,61,957 
only have been charged to the Income and Expenditure Account of the current year 
which would have been `5,14,21,519 on the basis of accounting practice followed 
earlier.
2.10	 Advance paid to IIT Bombay for Hallmarking Digitization Solution: An agreement 
was signed with IIT Bombay on 06.08.2019 for Hallmarking Digitization Solution for 
BIS at total cost of `7423.25 lakh. An advance of `1483.88 lakh was paid by BIS to 
IIT Bombay on 20.08.2019 as per the agreement [refer Schedule 7(B) Item 2(a)(i)]. 
However, later on, it was decided to discontinue the said project with IIT Bombay and a 
notice of 90 days for termination of agreement was sent to IIT Bombay on 28.02.2020 
in terms of para 16 and para 24 of the agreement. IIT Bombay has been requested to 
refund the money to Bureau.
2.11	 Recruitment Receipts: A recruitment process to recruit 109 Scientist B in BIS was 
initiated during 2017-18 and an amount of `303,46,398 was received towards 
application fee during financial year 2017-18 and 2018-19 and reflected in Schedule 
13 Item (h) as Recruitment Receipts. However, the recruitment process was cancelled 
as per the directions of the Deptt. of Consumer Affairs, Ministry of Consumer Affairs, 
Food and Public Distribution vide order No. 11/16/2018-BIS dated 13 April 2018.
	
The application fees collected was refunded during the year 2019-20 and has been 
shown under Refund of Recruitment Receipts in Schedule 15, Item 6(e).

2.13	 Surplus in General Provident Fund Accounts: There was a surplus (i.e. excess of 
income over expenditure) of `1,29,89,689 in BIS Employees General Provident 
Fund Accounts during 2019-20. This surplus is due to higher yield earned by BIS on 
investments of GPF as compared to the interest credited to subscribers Accounts. This 
has been treated as income of the Bureau as per the Accounting Policy [Schedule 13 
Item (g)].
2.14	 Income-Tax Exemption:
2.14.1	 Central Board of Direct Taxes(CBDT), Department of Revenue, Ministry of Finance, 
Govt. of India, vide its Notification No. 88/2014 dated 23.12.2014 had notified BIS 
under Section 10 (46) of Income-tax Act, 1961 for the Assessment Years 2012-13 to 
2016-17. The CBDT has further notified BIS under this section for the Assessment 
Years 2017-18 to 2021-22 vide Notification No. 111/2016 dated 01.12.2016. As a 
result of this notification of CBDT, the income of BIS is not taxable till Assessment Year 
2021-22.
2.14.2	 Prior to Assessment Year 2012-13, Income-tax Exemption was granted to BIS under 
section 10(23)(c)(iv) which was withdrawn by DG:IT(E) vide order dated 24.02.2012 
from Assessment Year 2009-10 and onwards. This was restored by DG:IT(E) vide 
order dated 04.12.2012 in compliance with the order of Hon’ble High Court of Delhi. 
Therefore, the tax exemption of BIS under section 10(23)(c)(iv) of Income Tax Act is 
also available for Assessment Years 2009-10 to 2011-12. The DG IT(E) had filed SLP in 
Hon’ble Supreme Court under Article 136 of the Constitution of India against allowing 
the Writ Petition of BIS by the Hon’ble High Court of Delhi. The SLP filed by DG:IT(E) 
has been converted into Civil Appeal which is pending in the Hon’ble Supreme Court.
2.15	 The Annual Accounts have been prepared in the Uniform Formats of Accounts 
prescribed by the Ministry of Finance.
2.16	 The previous year figures have been regrouped wherever found necessary to make 
them comparable with current year groups and figures.
2.17	 Figures in Final Accounts have been rounded off to the nearest rupee.

We have audited the attached Balance Sheet of the Bureau of Indian Standards (BIS), New 
Delhi as at 31 March 2020, Income & Expenditure Account and Receipts & Payments Account 
for the year ended on that date under Section 19 (2) of the Comptroller & Auditor General’s 
(Duties, Powers & Conditions of Service) Act, 1971 read with Section 24(2) of the Bureau 
of Indian Standards Act 2016.  These financial statements include the accounts of twenty 
four Branch Offices, four Regional Offices, Central Laboratory at Sahibabad and the National 
Institute for Training on Standardization (NITS) at Noida. These financial statements are the 
responsibility of the BIS’s management. Our responsibility is to express an opinion on these 
financial statements based on our audit.
2.	
This Separate Audit Report (SAR) contains the comments of the Comptroller & Auditor 
General of India (CAG) on the accounting treatment only with regard to classification, 
conformity with the best accounting practices, accounting standards and disclosure 
norms, etc. Audit observations on financial transactions with regard to compliance 
with the Laws, Rules & Regulations (Propriety and Regularity) and efficiency-cum-
performance aspects, etc., if any, are reported through Inspection Reports/CAG’s Audit 
Reports separately.
3.	
We have conducted our audit in accordance with auditing standards generally accepted 
in India. These standards require that we plan and perform the audit to obtain reasonable 
assurance about whether the financial statements are free from material misstatements. 
An audit includes examining, on a test basis, evidences supporting the amounts and 
disclosures in the financial statements. An audit also includes assessing the accounting 
principles used and significant estimates made by management, as well as evaluating 
the overall presentation of financial statements. We believe that our audit provides a 
reasonable basis for our opinion. 
4.	
Based on our audit, we report that:
	
(i)	 We have obtained all the information and explanations, which to the best of our 
knowledge and belief were necessary for the purpose of our audit.
	
(ii)	 The Balance Sheet, Income & Expenditure/Receipts & Payments Account dealt with 
by this report have been drawn up in the Uniform Format of Accounts as prescribed by 
the Ministry of Finance.
	
(iii)	In our opinion, proper books of accounts and other relevant records have been 
maintained, under section 24(1) of The Bureau of Indian Standards Act 2016, in so far as 
it appears from our examination of such books.
	
(iv)	We further report that:
A.   Balance Sheet
Liabilities
Current Liability and Provision (Schedule 3) – Rs. 28.44 Crore
No liability was provided for the unpaid Goods and Service Tax of Rs.96.16 lakh for the month 
of March 2020 pertaining to Branch Office, Ahmedabad. Further, an amount of Rs.0.42 lakh 
towards freight services availed during the year 2019-20 was paid in May 2020 by Branch 
Office, Jaipur and no liability was provided in the books of Accounts of BIS. This has resulted 
in understatement of liabilities and expenditure to the extent of Rs.96.58 lakh.
Separate Audit Report of the Comptroller & Auditor 
General of India on the Accounts of Bureau of Indian 
Standards for the year ended 31 March 2020.

B.  Income & Expenditure Account
Expenditure
C.   Receipts & Payments Account
The Receipts & Payments Account was not properly drawn as detailed below:-
•	
In the Receipts side, receipts under difference heads were not depicted separately as 
amounts of Services, Sales and Misc. are clubbed and shown under single head ‘Income-
Services, Sales and Miscellaneous’.
•	
In the Payments side, figures of Current Assets, Current Liabilities and Inter Accounts 
were netted and shown under the head ‘Other Payments’. Netting in accounts is not 
permissible in Uniform Format of Accounts. 
D.    Significant Accounting Policies (Schedule 16)
As per Significant Accounting Policies, (Point no.1. Accounting convention); accounts 
are prepared on the accrual basis of accounting. As per point no.8., the accounts of pay & 
allowances and leave encashment are being maintained on cash basis. This Accounting Policy, 
to the extent of maintenance of accounts of pay & allowances and leave encashment on cash 
basis, is not in conformity with the Uniform format of accounts.
E.   General:
E.1 	Consolidated Fixed Assets Register was not maintained.  Fixed Assets Register of BIS 
Headquarter was not maintained in the format of GFR Rule 40 and did not include 
assets like land, building and residential flats. Cumulative balance of item wise Fixed 
Assets could not be tallied with the accounts. Further, BIS also has possession of land on 
lease basis; however segregation of land on freehold and lease basis was not depicted in 
Accounts.
E.2	 As per Uniform Format of Accounts, Furniture and Fixtures, Office equipment and 
Computer/peripherals are to be shown separately since these items have different 
rates of depreciation. In Schedule 4 - Fixed Assets, actual amount of each item was not 
reflected as these items were clubbed and shown under single head A.6 – Furniture, 
Office Equipments & Computers. The same should be disclosed separately.
E.3 	Scrutiny of the bank reconciliation statements revealed the following discrepancies as 
on 31 March 2020:-
•	
Cheques issued but not encashed amounting to Rs. 22,678.00 pertaining to period 
February 2019 to October 2019 have become time barred.  This should be written 
back and shown as liability in the accounts 
•	
An amount of Rs. 2,12,797.11 pertaining to period June 2019 to March 2020 has 
been shown as cheques/DDs deposited in bank by BIS but not yet cleared/credited.
In addition to the above, cheques amounting to Rs. 43,79,723.36 of Bank of India & Canara 
Bank were found as unlink bank credit and  cheque of Rs 1,09,411.00 of  Canara bank was 
found as unlink bank debit.   
The same needs to be linked immediately with respective transactions and should be properly 
accounted for in the books of accounts. 
E.4  Status of Land (owned/leasehold) amounting to Rs.105.63 crore was not disclosed in 
Notes to Accounts.
E.5 	BIS received grant of Rs.2.00 crore with a condition that 10 per cent of the grant was to 
be spent for programme in North Eastern Region (NER).  However, separate accounts 
for expenses incurred in NER were not maintained. In the absence of separate accounts 
for NER, audit was not able to verify the actual expenditure incurred in NER. Further, 
out of grant of Rs.2.00 crore, Rs.1.00 crore was for ‘National System of Standardization’ 
and Rs.1.00 crore was for Gold Hallmarking Centre.  However, no expenditure has been 
made in NER.
E. 6   No liability has been provided by BIS for unspent amount of Rs.1.56 crore.
F.  Grants-in Aid
During 2019-20, Grants-in-aid of Rs.2.00 crore were received for setting up of Hallmarking 
Centres and for quality infrastructure for consumer protection from the Ministry of 
Consumer Affairs, Food & Public Distribution. It had Rs.0.04 crore as interest income and 
unspent balance of Rs.7.02 crore of the previous year. Out of total available amount of 
Rs.9.06 crore, a sum of Rs.1.07 crore was utilized during the year leaving a balance of Rs.7.99 
crore at the end of the year. Out of unutilised balance of Grants-in-Aid amounting to Rs.7.99 
crore, an amount of Rs.6.43 crore has been paid as advance to CPWD for upgradation and 
modernisation of NITS building under Plan project. Thus, the unspent balance is Rs.1.56 
crore. 
G. Management Letter
Deficiencies which have not been included in the Audit Report have been brought to the 
notice of Managing Director, Bureau of Indian Standards through a management letter 
issued separately for remedial/corrective action.
v. 	 Subject to our observations in the preceding paragraphs, we report that the Balance 
Sheet, Income & Expenditure Account and Receipts & Payments Account dealt with by 
this report are in agreement with the books of accounts.
vi.	 In our opinion and to the best of our information and according to the explanations given 
to us, the said financial statements read together with Notes on Accounts, and subject 
to the significant matters stated above and other matters mentioned in Annexure to 
this, the Audit Report gives a true and fair view in conformity with accounting principles 
generally accepted in India;
a.	
In so far as it relates to the Balance Sheet, of the state of affairs of the Bureau of 
Indian Standards as at 31 March 2020; and
b.	
In so far as it relates to Income and Expenditure Account of the Surplus for the year 
ended on that date.

Annexure
1.	
Adequacy of Internal audit system
	
Internal audit has been conducted by Chartered Accountant firm up to 31 March 2020.
2.	
Adequacy of Internal Control System
	
The internal control system of BIS needs to be strengthened as:
(i)	 Fixed Assets register was not properly maintained as per prescribed format.
(ii)	 Sundry Creditors towards TA/DA of Rs.59.46 lakh pertaining to the period 2007-08 
to 2018-19 were outstanding as on 31 March 2020. 
(iii)	 Sundry Debtors amounting to Rs. 36.13 lakh pertaining to the period prior to 2017-
18 to 2019-20 were outstanding as on 31 March 2020.
(iv)	 Advances amounting to Rs.13.90 crore for the period 2006-07 to 2018-19 were 
outstanding as on 31 March 2020.
(v)	 24 Paras of Inspection Reports for the period 2002-03 to 2014-17 were outstanding.
3.	
System of physical verification of assets
	
The physical verification of fixed assets of BIS Headquarters, New Delhi was conducted 
upto 31 March 2020 and no material deficiency reported.
4.	
System of physical verification of inventory
(i)	 Physical verification of Stationery and Consumables was conducted upto 31 March 
2020.
(ii)	 Physical verification of Books and Publications of BIS was conducted upto F.Y.2018-
19.
5. 	 Regularity in payment of dues
	
As per accounts, no payment over six months in respect of statutory dues was outstanding 
as on 31 March 2020.
